The OUTER circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary: the pipe casing exchanges heat with the environment at $u_{amb} = 298$ K through a Robin condition with effective convective heat transfer coefficient $h = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K).  The INNER circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — the tube wall whose shape is optimised — swept by the heating medium at temperature $u_H = 338$ K, which injects heat by convection through a Robin condition with heat transfer coefficient $h_H = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K) — the design-dependent convection setup of Iga et al., representative of the water side of a heating network.  The optimisation maximises the heat rejected to the environment through $\\Gamma_o$.\nThe Lagrangian of the convectively-heated pipe problem is\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal L(\\left[ u\\right],\\left[ \\delta u\\right]) = J(u) +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwith\n\\begin{align*}\nJ(u) &=  \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n \\end{align*}\nwhere the outer-surface temperature term is weighted by $q_b = h_H \\left( u_H - u_{amb}\\right) = 25 \\times 40 = 10^{3}$ W/m$^{2}$ — the flux the inner wall would transfer to ambient if the surface reached ambient temperature; minimizing $J$ therefore maximizes the heat rejected through the casing.  The Lagrange multiplier $\\ell = 5 \\times 10^{3}$ W K/m$^{3}$ weights the material cost; it is the dominant term, so the optimiser keeps only the material whose temperature reduction pays for itself, and the final design is a sparse conducting structure.\nThe state $\\left[ u\\right] \\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)$ is the solution of the linear problem\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u \\delta u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u_{H} \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u_{amb} \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} .\n\\end{dmath*}\nThe adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$ to the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ is the solution of\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere the adjoint load $\\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ comes from the partial derivative $\\partial_{u} J(\\delta u) = - \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ of the outer-surface temperature term in the objective.\nIn order to compute the shape derivative, we consider a shape perturbation $\\mathbf x = T_t(\\mathbf X) = \\mathbf X + t \\mathbf  V(\\mathbf X)$ with a suitable velocity field $\\mathbf V$, vanishing on the fixed outer circle.  The perturbed Lagrangian $\\mathcal G$ using the Lagrangian state $\\left[ u\\right]^{L}$ is given by\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal G(t,\\left[ u\\right]^{L},\\left[ \\delta u\\right]^{L}) = \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}  \\operatorname{det}\\mathbf F( t )  \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u^{L}\\right) \\,ds_x +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}  \\operatorname{det}\\mathbf F( t )  \\left( \\left(  \\nabla u^{L} \\cdot  \\mathbf F^{-1}(t) \\right) \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u^{L} \\cdot  \\mathbf F^{-1}(t) \\right) \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u^{L} - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u^{L} \\,J_{s}(t) \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u^{L} - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u^{L} \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere $\\mathbf F(t) = \\mathbf I + t \\nabla \\mathbf V$ is the deformation gradient and $J_{s}(t)$ the boundary Jacobian of the moving inner boundary $\\Gamma_i$; the fixed casing $\\Gamma_o$ carries no Jacobian because $\\mathbf V$ vanishes there.\nFor the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ and the adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$, the volume expression of the shape derivative, including the curvature term on the moving inner boundary, is given by\n\\begin{dmath*}\nDJ(\\Omega)(\\mathbf V) = \\int_{D} \\left(\\operatorname{div} {\\mathbf V} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) - \\left(  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla {\\mathbf V}  \\right) \\right) - \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\cdot  \\nabla {\\mathbf V}  \\right) \\right)\\right) k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\operatorname{div} {\\mathbf V} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} \\kappa h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\tilde u \\left(\\mathbf V \\cdot \\mathbf n\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere $\\kappa$ is the curvature of $\\Gamma_i$ and $\\mathbf n$ its outward unit normal.  The inner circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — its shape is optimised — while the outer circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary whose position is prescribed by the pipe geometry.\nThe initial design is the FULL annulus with four circular holes of radius $0.08$ cut at the mid-radius $0.325$ on the four axes — a rotationally symmetric seed that covers both the heating wall and the casing while giving the optimiser enclosed interfaces to work from,\n\\begin{align*}\n\\phi_0(x,y) = \\max\\left( 0.08 - \\sqrt{\\left( x - 0.325 \\right)^{2} + y^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{ x^{2} + \\left( y - 0.325 \\right)^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{\\left( x + 0.325 \\right)^{2} + y^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{ x^{2} + \\left( y + 0.325 \\right)^{2}} \\right).\n\\end{align*}\n\\end{document}\n\n\n=== heatPipeHeatingRRParameter.tex ===\n\\documentclass[preview]{standalone}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{setspace}\n\\usepackage{bm}\n\\let\\oldcdot\\cdot\n\\usepackage{breqn}\n\\let\\cdot\\oldcdot\n\\begin{document}\n\\begin{spacing}{1.2}\n\\begin{tabular}{cccl}\n\\toprule\nthermal conductivity (aluminium) & $k_0$ & 200 & $\\frac{W}{K m}$ \\\nthermal conductivity (air) & $k_{air}$ & 0.026 & $\\frac{W}{K m}$ \\\nconvective heat transfer coefficient (environment) & $h$ & 25 & $\\frac{W}{m^2 K}$ \\\nambient temperature & $u_{amb}$ & 298 & $K$ \\\nconvective heat transfer coefficient (heating side) & $h_H$ & 25 & $\\frac{W}{m^2 K}$ \\\nheating-medium temperature & $u_H$ & 338 & $K$ \\\nLagrange multiplier & $\\ell$ & $5 \\times 10^3$ & $\\frac{W\\,K}{m^3}$ \\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{spacing}\n\\end{document}\n\n\n=== mesh.txt ===\nreal R = 0.5; The inner circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — its shape is optimised — while the outer circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary whose position is prescribed by the pipe geometry.\nThe initial design is the FULL annulus with four circular holes of radius $0.08$ cut at the mid-radius $0.325$ on the four axes — a rotationally symmetric seed that covers both the heating wall and the casing while giving the optimiser enclosed interfaces to work from,\n\\begin{align*}\n\\phi_0(x,y) = \\max\\left( 0.08 - \\sqrt{\\left( x - 0.325 \\right)^{2} + y^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{ x^{2} + \\left( y - 0.325 \\right)^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{\\left( x + 0.325 \\right)^{2} + y^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{ x^{2} + \\left( y + 0.325 \\right)^{2}} \\right).\n\\end{align*}\n\\end{document}\n\n\n=== FILE: heatPipeHeatingRRParameter.tex ===\n\\documentclass[preview]{standalone}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{setspace}\n\\usepackage{bm}\n\\let\\oldcdot\\cdot\n\\usepackage{breqn}\n\\let\\cdot\\oldcdot\n\\begin{document}\n\\begin{spacing}{1.2}\n\\begin{tabular}{cccl}\n\\toprule\nthermal conductivity (aluminium) & $k_0$ & 200 & $\\frac{W}{K m}$ \\\nthermal conductivity (air) & $k_{air}$ & 0.026 & $\\frac{W}{K m}$ \\\nconvective heat transfer coefficient (environment) & $h$ & 25 & $\\frac{W}{m^2 K}$ \\\nambient temperature & $u_{amb}$ & 298 & $K$ \\\nconvective heat transfer coefficient (heating side) & $h_H$ & 25 & $\\frac{W}{m^2 K}$ \\\nheating-medium temperature & $u_H$ & 338 & $K$ \\\nLagrange multiplier & $\\ell$ & $5 \\times 10^3$ & $\\frac{W\\,K}{m^3}$ \\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{spacing}\n\\end{document}\n\n\n=== FILE: mesh.txt ===\nreal R = 0.5;    // outer radius of the pipe cross-section (fixed, diameter 1 m)\nreal r0 = 0.15;  // initial inner radius (the inner boundary is a design boundary)\nborder C01(t=0, 2*pi){x=R*cos(t); y=R*sin(t); label=3;}   // outer circle, Robin environment (fixed)\nborder C02(t=0, 2*pi){x=r0*cos(t); The INNER circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — the tube wall whose shape is optimised — swept by the heating medium at temperature $u_H = 338$ K, which injects heat by convection through a Robin condition with heat transfer coefficient $h_H = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K) — the design-dependent convection setup of Iga et al., representative of the water side of a heating network. The perturbed Lagrangian $\\mathcal G$ using the Lagrangian state $\\left[ u\\right]^{L}$ is given by\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal G(t,\\left[ u\\right]^{L},\\left[ \\delta u\\right]^{L}) = \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}  \\operatorname{det}\\mathbf F( t )  \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u^{L}\\right) \\,ds_x +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}  \\operatorname{det}\\mathbf F( t )  \\left( \\left(  \\nabla u^{L} \\cdot  \\mathbf F^{-1}(t) \\right) \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u^{L} \\cdot  \\mathbf F^{-1}(t) \\right) \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u^{L} - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u^{L} \\,J_{s}(t) \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u^{L} - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u^{L} \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere $\\mathbf F(t) = \\mathbf I + t \\nabla \\mathbf V$ is the deformation gradient and $J_{s}(t)$ the boundary Jacobian of the moving inner boundary $\\Gamma_i$; the fixed casing $\\Gamma_o$ carries no Jacobian because $\\mathbf V$ vanishes there.\nFor the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ and the adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$, the volume expression of the shape derivative, including the curvature term on the moving inner boundary, is given by\n\\begin{dmath*}\nDJ(\\Omega)(\\mathbf V) = \\int_{D} \\left(\\operatorname{div} {\\mathbf V} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) - \\left(  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla {\\mathbf V}  \\right) \\right) - \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\cdot  \\nabla {\\mathbf V}  \\right) \\right)\\right) k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\operatorname{div} {\\mathbf V} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} \\kappa h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\tilde u \\left(\\mathbf V \\cdot \\mathbf n\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere $\\kappa$ is the curvature of $\\Gamma_i$ and $\\mathbf n$ its outward unit normal.  The inner circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — its shape is optimised — while the outer circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary whose position is prescribed by the pipe geometry.\nThe initial design is the FULL annulus with four circular holes of radius $0.08$ cut at the mid-radius $0.325$ on the four axes — a rotationally symmetric seed that covers both the heating wall and the casing while giving the optimiser enclosed interfaces to work from,\n\\begin{align*}\n\\phi_0(x,y) = \\max\\left( 0.08 - \\sqrt{\\left( x - 0.325 \\right)^{2} + y^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{ x^{2} + \\left( y - 0.325 \\right)^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{\\left( x + 0.325 \\right)^{2} + y^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{ x^{2} + \\left( y + 0.325 \\right)^{2}} \\right).\n\\end{align*}\n\\end{document}\n\n\n=== FILE: The OUTER circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary: the pipe casing exchanges heat with the environment at $u_{amb} = 298$ K through a Robin condition with effective convective heat transfer coefficient $h = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K).  The INNER circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — the tube wall whose shape is optimised — swept by the heating medium at temperature $u_H = 338$ K, which injects heat by convection through a Robin condition with heat transfer coefficient $h_H = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K) — the design-dependent convection setup of Iga et al., representative of the water side of a heating network.  The optimisation maximises the heat rejected to the environment through $\\Gamma_o$.\nThe Lagrangian of the convectively-heated pipe problem is\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal L(\\left[ u\\right],\\left[ \\delta u\\right]) = J(u) +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwith\n\\begin{align*}\nJ(u) &=  \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n \\end{align*}\nwhere the outer-surface temperature term is weighted by $q_b = h_H \\left( u_H - u_{amb}\\right) = 25 \\times 40 = 10^{3}$ W/m$^{2}$ — the flux the inner wall would transfer to ambient if the surface reached ambient temperature; minimizing $J$ therefore maximizes the heat rejected through the casing.  The Lagrange multiplier $\\ell = 5 \\times 10^{3}$ W K/m$^{3}$ weights the material cost; it is the dominant term, so the optimiser keeps only the material whose temperature reduction pays for itself, and the final design is a sparse conducting structure.\nThe state $\\left[ u\\right] \\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)$ is the solution of the linear problem\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u \\delta u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u_{H} \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u_{amb} \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} .\n\\end{dmath*}\nThe adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$ to the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ is the solution of\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere the adjoint load $\\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ comes from the partial derivative $\\partial_{u} J(\\delta u) = - \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ of the outer-surface temperature term in the objective.\nIn order to compute the shape derivative, we consider a shape perturbation $\\mathbf x = T_t(\\mathbf X) = \\mathbf X + t \\mathbf  V(\\mathbf X)$ with a suitable velocity field $\\mathbf V$, vanishing on the fixed outer circle.  The perturbed Lagrangian $\\mathcal G$ using the Lagrangian state $\\left[ u\\right]^{L}$ is given by\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal G(t,\\left[ u\\right]^{L},\\left[ \\delta u\\right]^{L}) = \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}  \\operatorname{det}\\mathbf F( t )  \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u^{L}\\right) \\,ds_x +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}  \\operatorname{det}\\mathbf F( t )  \\left( \\left(  \\nabla u^{L} \\cdot  \\mathbf F^{-1}(t) \\right) \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u^{L} \\cdot  \\mathbf F^{-1}(t) \\right) \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u^{L} - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u^{L} \\,J_{s}(t) \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u^{L} - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u^{L} \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere $\\mathbf F(t) = \\mathbf I + t \\nabla \\mathbf V$ is the deformation gradient and $J_{s}(t)$ the boundary Jacobian of the moving inner boundary $\\Gamma_i$; the fixed casing $\\Gamma_o$ carries no Jacobian because $\\mathbf V$ vanishes there.\nFor the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ and the adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$, the volume expression of the shape derivative, including the curvature term on the moving inner boundary, is given by\n\\begin{dmath*}\nDJ(\\Omega)(\\mathbf V) = \\int_{D} \\left(\\operatorname{div} {\\mathbf V} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) - \\left(  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla {\\mathbf V}  \\right) \\right) - \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\cdot  \\nabla {\\mathbf V}  \\right) \\right)\\right) k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\operatorname{div} {\\mathbf V} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} \\kappa h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\tilde u \\left(\\mathbf V \\cdot \\mathbf n\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere $\\kappa$ is the curvature of $\\Gamma_i$ and $\\mathbf n$ its outward unit normal.  The inner circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — its shape is optimised — while the outer circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary whose position is prescribed by the pipe geometry.\nThe initial design is the FULL annulus with four circular holes of radius $0.08$ cut at the mid-radius $0.325$ on the four axes — a rotationally symmetric seed that covers both the heating wall and the casing while giving the optimiser enclosed interfaces to work from,\n\\begin{align*}\n\\phi_0(x,y) = \\max\\left( 0.08 - \\sqrt{\\left( x - 0.325 \\right)^{2} + y^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{ x^{2} + \\left( y - 0.325 \\right)^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{\\left( x + 0.325 \\right)^{2} + y^{2}} ,\\ 0.08 - \\sqrt{ x^{2} + \\left( y + 0.325 \\right)^{2}} \\right).\n\\end{align*}\n\\end{document}\n\n\n=== heatPipeHeatingRRParameter.tex ===\n\\documentclass[preview]{standalone}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{setspace}\n\\usepackage{bm}\n\\let\\oldcdot\\cdot\n\\usepackage{breqn}\n\\let\\cdot\\oldcdot\n\\begin{document}\n\\begin{spacing}{1.2}\n\\begin{tabular}{cccl}\n\\toprule\nthermal conductivity (aluminium) & $k_0$ & 200 & $\\frac{W}{K m}$ \\\nthermal conductivity (air) & $k_{air}$ & 0.026 & $\\frac{W}{K m}$ \\\nconvective heat transfer coefficient (environment) & $h$ & 25 & $\\frac{W}{m^2 K}$ \\\nambient temperature & $u_{amb}$ & 298 & $K$ \\\nconvective heat transfer coefficient (heating side) & $h_H$ & 25 & $\\frac{W}{m^2 K}$ \\\nheating-medium temperature & $u_H$ & 338 & $K$ \\\nLagrange multiplier & $\\ell$ & $5 \\times 10^3$ & $\\frac{W\\,K}{m^3}$ \\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{spacing}\n\\end{document}\n\n\n=== tex/pipeheatingRR.tex ===\n\\documentclass[preview]{standalone}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{setspace}\n\\usepackage{bm}\n\\let\\oldcdot\\cdot\n\\usepackage{breqn}\n\\let\\cdot\\oldcdot\n\\begin{document}\nLet $\\phi(x)$ be the level-set function and\n \\begin{align*}\\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}(x) &= \\begin{cases} 1 & \\mbox{if}\\quad \\phi(x) < 0 \\\\ 0.00013 & \\mbox{if}\\quad \\phi(x)  \\ge 0 \\end{cases}.\\end{align*}\nThe Lagrangian of the pipe heating RR problem is\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal L(\\left[ u\\right],\\left[ \\delta u\\right]) = J(u) - ===\n\\documentclass[preview]{standalone}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{setspace}\n\\usepackage{bm}\n\\let\\oldcdot\\cdot\n\\usepackage{breqn}\n\\let\\cdot\\oldcdot\n\\begin{document}\nLet $\\phi(x)$ be the level-set function and\n \\begin{align*}\\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}(x) &= \\begin{cases} 1 & \\mbox{if}\\quad \\phi(x) < 0 \\\\ k_{air}/k_0 & \\mbox{if}\\quad \\phi(x)  \\ge 0 \\end{cases},\\end{align*}\nwhere the weak-material contrast is the physically motivated ratio of the thermal conductivity of air to that of aluminium, $k_{air}/k_0 = 0.026/200 \\approx 1.3 \\times 10^{-4}$ (numerically $1.3 \\times 10^{-4}$, the value taken by the characteristic function).\nThe design domain is the annular cross-section of a district-heating pipe, $D = \\left\\{ \\mathbf x : r_0 < \\left|\\mathbf x\\right| < R \\right\\}$ with outer radius $R = 0.5$ m (a pipe of diameter 1 m, scaled to unit size for benchmark comparison) and initial inner radius $r_0 = 0.15$ m.  The OUTER circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary: the pipe casing exchanges heat with the environment at $u_{amb} = 298$ K through a Robin condition with effective convective heat transfer coefficient $h = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K).  The INNER circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — the tube wall whose shape is optimised — swept by the heating medium at temperature $u_H = 338$ K, which injects heat by convection through a Robin condition with heat transfer coefficient $h_H = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K) — the design-dependent convection setup of Iga et al., representative of the water side of a heating network.  The optimisation maximises the heat rejected to the environment through $\\Gamma_o$.\nThe Lagrangian of the convectively-heated pipe problem is\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal L(\\left[ u\\right],\\left[ \\delta u\\right]) = J(u) +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwith\n\\begin{align*}\nJ(u) &=  \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n \\end{align*}\nwhere the outer-surface temperature term is weighted by $q_b = h_H \\left( u_H - u_{amb}\\right) = 25 \\times 40 = 10^{3}$ W/m$^{2}$ — the flux the inner wall would transfer to ambient if the surface reached ambient temperature; minimizing $J$ therefore maximizes the heat rejected through the casing.  The Lagrange multiplier $\\ell = 5 \\times 10^{3}$ W K/m$^{3}$ weights the material cost; it is the dominant term, so the optimiser keeps only the material whose temperature reduction pays for itself, and the final design is a sparse conducting structure.\nThe state $\\left[ u\\right] \\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)$ is the solution of the linear problem\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u \\delta u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u_{H} \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u_{amb} \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} .\n\\end{dmath*}\nThe adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$ to the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ is the solution of\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere the adjoint load $\\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ comes from the partial derivative $\\partial_{u} J(\\delta u) = - \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ of the outer-surface temperature term in the objective.\nIn order to compute the shape derivative, we consider a shape perturbation $\\mathbf x = T_t(\\mathbf X) = \\mathbf X + t \\mathbf  V(\\mathbf X)$ with a suitable velocity field $\\mathbf V$, vanishing on the fixed outer circle.  The perturbed Lagrangian $\\mathcal G$ using the Lagrangian state $\\left[ u\\right]^{L}$ is given by\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal G(t,\\left[ u\\right]^{L},\\left[ \\delta u\\right]^{L}) = \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}  \\operatorname{det}\\mathbf F( t )  \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u^{L}\\right) \\,ds_x +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}  \\operatorname{det}\\mathbf F( t )  \\left( \\left(  \\nabla u^{L} \\cdot  \\mathbf F^{-1}(t) \\right) \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u^{L} \\cdot  \\mathbf F^{-1}(t) \\right) \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u^{L} - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u^{L} \\,J_{s}(t) \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u^{L} - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u^{L} \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere $\\mathbf F(t) = \\mathbf I + t \\nabla \\mathbf V$ is the deformation gradient and $J_{s}(t)$ the boundary Jacobian of the moving inner boundary $\\Gamma_i$; the fixed casing $\\Gamma_o$ carries no Jacobian because $\\mathbf V$ vanishes there.\nFor the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ and the adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$, the volume expression of the shape derivative, including the curvature term on the moving inner boundary, is given by\n\\begin{dmath*}\nDJ(\\Omega)(\\mathbf V) = \\int_{D} \\left(\\operatorname{div} {\\mathbf V} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) - \\left(  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla {\\mathbf V}  \\right) \\right) - \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot \\left(  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\cdot  \\nabla {\\mathbf V}  \\right) \\right)\\right) k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\operatorname{div} {\\mathbf V} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} \\kappa h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\tilde u \\left(\\mathbf V \\cdot \\mathbf n\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere $\\kappa$ is the curvature of $\\Gamma_i$ and $\\mathbf n$ its outward unit normal. ===\n\\documentclass[preview]{standalone}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{setspace}\n\\usepackage{bm}\n\\let\\oldcdot\\cdot\n\\usepackage{breqn}\n\\let\\cdot\\oldcdot\n\\begin{document}\nLet $\\phi(x)$ be the level-set function and\n \\begin{align*}\\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$}(x) &= \\begin{cases} 1 & \\mbox{if}\\quad \\phi(x) < 0 \\\\ k_{air}/k_0 & \\mbox{if}\\quad \\phi(x)  \\ge 0 \\end{cases},\\end{align*}\nwhere the weak-material contrast is the physically motivated ratio of the thermal conductivity of air to that of aluminium, $k_{air}/k_0 = 0.026/200 \\approx 1.3 \\times 10^{-4}$ (numerically $1.3 \\times 10^{-4}$, the value taken by the characteristic function).\nThe design domain is the annular cross-section of a district-heating pipe, $D = \\left\\{ \\mathbf x : r_0 < \\left|\\mathbf x\\right| < R \\right\\}$ with outer radius $R = 0.5$ m (a pipe of diameter 1 m, scaled to unit size for benchmark comparison) and initial inner radius $r_0 = 0.15$ m.  The OUTER circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary: the pipe casing exchanges heat with the environment at $u_{amb} = 298$ K through a Robin condition with effective convective heat transfer coefficient $h = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K).  The INNER circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — the tube wall whose shape is optimised — swept by the heating medium at temperature $u_H = 338$ K, which injects heat by convection through a Robin condition with heat transfer coefficient $h_H = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K) — the design-dependent convection setup of Iga et al., representative of the water side of a heating network.  The optimisation maximises the heat rejected to the environment through $\\Gamma_o$.\nThe Lagrangian of the convectively-heated pipe problem is\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal L(\\left[ u\\right],\\left[ \\delta u\\right]) = J(u) +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwith\n\\begin{align*}\nJ(u) &=  \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n \\end{align*}\nwhere the outer-surface temperature term is weighted by $q_b = h_H \\left( u_H - u_{amb}\\right) = 25 \\times 40 = 10^{3}$ W/m$^{2}$ — the flux the inner wall would transfer to ambient if the surface reached ambient temperature; minimizing $J$ therefore maximizes the heat rejected through the casing.  The Lagrange multiplier $\\ell = 5 \\times 10^{3}$ W K/m$^{3}$ weights the material cost; it is the dominant term, so the optimiser keeps only the material whose temperature reduction pays for itself, and the final design is a sparse conducting structure.\nThe state $\\left[ u\\right] \\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)$ is the solution of the linear problem\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u \\delta u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u_{H} \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u_{amb} \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} .\n\\end{dmath*}\nThe adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$ to the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ is the solution of\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere the adjoint load $\\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ comes from the partial derivative $\\partial_{u} J(\\delta u) = - \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ of the outer-surface temperature term in the objective.\nIn order to compute the shape derivative, we consider a shape perturbation $\\mathbf x = T_t(\\mathbf X) = \\mathbf X + t \\mathbf  V(\\mathbf X)$ with a suitable velocity field $\\mathbf V$, vanishing on the fixed outer circle. The OUTER circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary: the pipe casing exchanges heat with the environment at $u_{amb} = 298$ K through a Robin condition with effective convective heat transfer coefficient $h = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K).  The INNER circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — the tube wall whose shape is optimised — swept by the heating medium at temperature $u_H = 338$ K, which injects heat by convection through a Robin condition with heat transfer coefficient $h_H = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K) — the design-dependent convection setup of Iga et al., representative of the water side of a heating network.  The optimisation maximises the heat rejected to the environment through $\\Gamma_o$.\nThe Lagrangian of the convectively-heated pipe problem is\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal L(\\left[ u\\right],\\left[ \\delta u\\right]) = J(u) +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwith\n\\begin{align*}\nJ(u) &=  \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n \\end{align*}\nwhere the outer-surface temperature term is weighted by $q_b = h_H \\left( u_H - u_{amb}\\right) = 25 \\times 40 = 10^{3}$ W/m$^{2}$ — the flux the inner wall would transfer to ambient if the surface reached ambient temperature; minimizing $J$ therefore maximizes the heat rejected through the casing.  The Lagrange multiplier $\\ell = 5 \\times 10^{3}$ W K/m$^{3}$ weights the material cost; it is the dominant term, so the optimiser keeps only the material whose temperature reduction pays for itself, and the final design is a sparse conducting structure.\nThe state $\\left[ u\\right] \\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)$ is the solution of the linear problem\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u \\delta u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u_{H} \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u_{amb} \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} .\n\\end{dmath*}\nThe adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$ to the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ is the solution of\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere the adjoint load $\\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ comes from the partial derivative $\\partial_{u} J(\\delta u) = - \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ of the outer-surface temperature term in the objective.\nIn order to compute the shape derivative, we consider a shape perturbation $\\mathbf x = T_t(\\mathbf X) = \\mathbf X + t \\mathbf  V(\\mathbf X)$ with a suitable velocity field $\\mathbf V$, vanishing on the fixed outer circle. The OUTER circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary: the pipe casing exchanges heat with the environment at $u_{amb} = 298$ K through a Robin condition with effective convective heat transfer coefficient $h = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K).  The INNER circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — the tube wall whose shape is optimised — swept by the heating medium at temperature $u_H = 338$ K, which injects heat by convection through a Robin condition with heat transfer coefficient $h_H = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K) — the design-dependent convection setup of Iga et al., representative of the water side of a heating network.  The optimisation maximises the heat rejected to the environment through $\\Gamma_o$.\nThe Lagrangian of the convectively-heated pipe problem is\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal L(\\left[ u\\right],\\left[ \\delta u\\right]) = J(u) +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwith\n\\begin{align*}\nJ(u) &=  \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n \\end{align*}\nwhere the outer-surface temperature term is weighted by $q_b = h_H \\left( u_H - u_{amb}\\right) = 25 \\times 40 = 10^{3}$ W/m$^{2}$ — the flux the inner wall would transfer to ambient if the surface reached ambient temperature; minimizing $J$ therefore maximizes the heat rejected through the casing.  The Lagrange multiplier $\\ell = 5 \\times 10^{3}$ W K/m$^{3}$ weights the material cost; it is the dominant term, so the optimiser keeps only the material whose temperature reduction pays for itself, and the final design is a sparse conducting structure.\nThe state $\\left[ u\\right] \\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)$ is the solution of the linear problem\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u \\delta u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u_{H} \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u_{amb} \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} .\n\\end{dmath*}\nThe adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$ to the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ is the solution of\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere the adjoint load $\\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ comes from the partial derivative $\\partial_{u} J(\\delta u) = - \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ of the outer-surface temperature term in the objective.\nIn order to compute the shape derivative, we consider a shape perturbation $\\mathbf x = T_t(\\mathbf X) = \\mathbf X + t \\mathbf  V(\\mathbf X)$ with a suitable velocity field $\\mathbf V$, vanishing on the fixed outer circle. ===\n\\documentclass[preview]{standalone}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{setspace}\n\\usepackage{bm}\n\\let\\oldcdot\\cdot\n\\usepackage{breqn}\n\\let\\cdot\\oldcdot\n\\begin{document}\nLet r_0 < \\left|\\mathbf x\\right| < R \\right\\}$ with outer radius $R = 0.5$ m (a pipe of diameter 1 m, scaled to unit size for benchmark comparison) and initial inner radius $r_0 = 0.15$ m.  The OUTER circle $\\Gamma_o$ is a fixed boundary: the pipe casing exchanges heat with the environment at $u_{amb} = 298$ K through a Robin condition with effective convective heat transfer coefficient $h = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K).  The INNER circle $\\Gamma_i$ is a design boundary — the tube wall whose shape is optimised — swept by the heating medium at temperature $u_H = 338$ K, which injects heat by convection through a Robin condition with heat transfer coefficient $h_H = 25$ W/(m$^{2}$ K) — the design-dependent convection setup of Iga et al., representative of the water side of a heating network.  The optimisation maximises the heat rejected to the environment through $\\Gamma_o$.\nThe Lagrangian of the convectively-heated pipe problem is\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\mathcal L(\\left[ u\\right],\\left[ \\delta u\\right]) = J(u) +  \\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\left( u - u_{H}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\left( u - u_{amb}\\right) \\delta u \\,ds_x ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwith\n\\begin{align*}\nJ(u) &=  \\int_{D} \\ell \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\left( u_{amb} - u\\right) \\,ds_x ,\n \\end{align*}\nwhere the outer-surface temperature term is weighted by $q_b = h_H \\left( u_H - u_{amb}\\right) = 25 \\times 40 = 10^{3}$ W/m$^{2}$ — the flux the inner wall would transfer to ambient if the surface reached ambient temperature; minimizing $J$ therefore maximizes the heat rejected through the casing.  The Lagrange multiplier $\\ell = 5 \\times 10^{3}$ W K/m$^{3}$ weights the material cost; it is the dominant term, so the optimiser keeps only the material whose temperature reduction pays for itself, and the final design is a sparse conducting structure.\nThe state $\\left[ u\\right] \\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)$ is the solution of the linear problem\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\delta u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u \\delta u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H u_{H} \\delta u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h u_{amb} \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} .\n\\end{dmath*}\nThe adjoint state $\\left[ \\tilde u\\right]$ to the direct state $\\left[ u\\right]$ is the solution of\n\\begin{dmath*}\n\\int_{D} k \\raisebox{\\depth}{$\\chi$} \\left(  \\nabla \\delta u  \\cdot  \\nabla \\tilde u  \\right) \\,dx + \\int_{\\Gamma_i} h_H \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x + \\int_{\\Gamma_o} h \\delta u \\tilde u \\,ds_x = \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x \\qquad \\forall\\; {\\delta u\\in \\mathbb P_{2}(D)} ,\n\\end{dmath*}\nwhere the adjoint load $\\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ comes from the partial derivative $\\partial_{u} J(\\delta u) = - \\int_{\\Gamma_o} q_b \\delta u \\,ds_x$ of the outer-surface temperature term in the objective.\nIn order to compute the shape derivative, we consider a shape perturbation $\\mathbf x = T_t(\\mathbf X) = \\mathbf X + t \\mathbf  V(\\mathbf X)$ with a suitable velocity field $\\mathbf V$, vanishing on the fixed outer circle.
